zero, two and six. Radial derivative cross terms vanish by angular
orthogonality. Finally,
\[
 \begin{aligned}
 \partial_\theta(B_{\rm aff}c+Cc^2)&=-\sin\theta(B_{\rm aff}+2C\cos\theta),\\
 \partial_\theta[\sin\theta(b+c\,d)]
            &=c\,b+(2c^2-1)d,\\
 \partial_\theta[\sin^2\theta\,T]&=2\sin\theta\,c\,T .
 \end{aligned}
\]
Substitution into (PG4), using (PG8), proves (PG7), including its
off-diagonal entries.

\subsection*{3. Explicit cap lower bounds of orders six, four and two}

Set \(\alpha_0=23/24\). For \(0\le\theta\le R\le1/2\),
\[
 \alpha_0\theta\le\sin\theta\le\theta,\qquad
 J_0\le R^3/3,\qquad I_0\le R^5/5.
 \tag{PG9}
\]
The lower sine bound follows from
\(\sin\theta\ge\theta-\theta^3/6\).
The scalar block can be completed exactly:
\[
 E_0=\frac{I_0}{4J_0}|B_{\rm aff}+2C I_1/I_0|^2
       +\frac{|C|^2}{J_0}(I_2-I_1^2/I_0).
 \tag{PG10}
\]
In particular the estimate below holds for the actual affine
coefficient \(B_{\rm aff}\); optimization in this identity does not replace it.
The covariance determinant is
\[
 I_0I_2-I_1^2=
 \frac12\int_0^R\int_0^R
 \sin^4\theta\sin^4\phi(\cos\theta-\cos\phi)^2\,d\theta\,d\phi
 \ \ge\ \frac{\alpha_0^{10}R^{14}}{2205}.
 \tag{PG11}
\]
For the inequality, integrate (PG9) between \(\theta\) and \(\phi\)
to obtain
\(|\cos\theta-\cos\phi|\ge
\alpha_0|\theta^2-\phi^2|/2\).
The remaining double integral is exactly
\[
 \int_0^R\int_0^R\theta^4\phi^4(\theta^2-\phi^2)^2\,d\theta\,d\phi
 =2R^{14}\left(\frac1{45}-\frac1{49}\right)
 =\frac{8R^{14}}{2205}.
\]
Combining (PG9)–(PG11) proves
\[
 E_0\ge\frac{\alpha_0^{10}R^6}{147}|C|^2
       =\frac{\alpha_0^{10}}{588}\,r^6\,\frac{|C|^2}{16}.
 \tag{PG12}
\]

For the vector block the two exact squared channels are
\[
 E_1=\frac1{12J_0}\int_0^R\sin^2\theta
 \bigl(|c\,b+(2c^2-1)d|^2+2|b+c\,d|^2\bigr)d\theta .
 \tag{PG13}
\]
For each component and each \(\theta\), the Gram determinant of
the two vectors \((c,\sqrt2)\) and \((2c^2-1,\sqrt2c)\) is
\(2\sin^4\theta\). Completing the square in \(b_\alpha\) yields
the pointwise lower bound
\(2\sin^4\theta|d_\alpha|^2/(c^2+2)\).
This is at least \(2\sin^4\theta|d_\alpha|^2/3\).
Since \(\int_0^R\sin^6\theta\,d\theta\ge\alpha_0^6R^7/7\),
\[
 E_1\ge\frac{\alpha_0^6R^4}{42}|d|^2
       =\frac{\alpha_0^6}{28}\,r^4\,\frac{|d|^2}{24}.
 \tag{PG14}
\]
The tensor block in (PG7) retains both contributions. Its exact
integrand contains \(4c^2+6\ge6\), so
\[
 E_2\ge\frac{\|T\|_{\rm HS}^2I_0}{5J_0}
       \ge\frac{3\alpha_0^4R^2}{25}\|T\|_{\rm HS}^2
       =\frac{9\alpha_0^4}{25}\,r^2
                       \frac{\|T\|_{\rm HS}^2}{12}.
 \tag{PG15}
\]
Define the exact positive constant
\[
 c_6=\frac{(23/24)^{10}}{588}
     =\frac{41426511213649}{37281188007641088}
     >2^{-10}.
 \tag{PG16}
\]
The two other constants in (PG14)–(PG15) exceed \(c_6\).
Since \(r\le1\), (PG3), (PG12), (PG14) and (PG15) imply
\[
 \frac1{v(r)}\int_{d(z,U)\le r}|\nabla P(U)|^2dU
                  \ge c_6r^6\|\Pi_2P\|_2^2
 \quad\text{for every }z\in SU(2).
 \tag{PG17}
\]
To prove the passage to every centre, left multiplication by \(z\)
is a real orthogonal linear map of the four quaternion coordinates.
It preserves the radius-two metric, Haar measure, harmonic degree
and the norm of \(\Pi_2P\). Apply the identity-centred proof to
\(U\mapsto P(zU)\). Its affine coefficients can change, but (PG10)
and (PG13) hold for every value of those coefficients.

\subsection*{4. A unit density with bounded score}

Let \(\rho\) be a positive \(C^1\) density with
\(\int\rho\,dU=1\) and \(|\nabla\log\rho|\le B\), \(B\ge0\),
in precisely the metric (PG4). The geodesic integral of its score
gives \(\rho(U)\ge e^{-Br}\rho(z)\) whenever \(d(U,z)\le r\).
Multiply (PG17) by \(\rho(z)\), and integrate in \(z\).
Fubini and the reverse estimate
\(\rho(z)\le e^{Br}\rho(U)\) give
\[
 \begin{aligned}
 c_6r^6\|\Pi_2P\|_2^2
 &\le\frac1{v(r)}\int|\nabla P(U)|^2
                      \int_{d(z,U)\le r}\rho(z)\,dz\,dU\\
 &\le e^{Br}\int|\nabla P(U)|^2\rho(U)\,dU .
 \end{aligned}\tag{PG18}
\]
The cap volume has cancelled exactly. Thus no dimension-three
volume penalty is required.

Choose \(r=2/(B+2)\), and put \(t=B/(B+2)\in[0,1)\).
Then \(r(1+B)=1+t\), \(Br=2t\), and
\(\log(1+t)\ge t/2\), by integrating
\((1+t)^{-1}\ge1/2\) over \([0,t]\). It follows that
\((1+t)^6e^{-2t}\ge1\). We have proved
\[
 \boxed{\displaystyle
 \int|\nabla P|^2\rho\,dU\ge
       \eta_6(B)\|\Pi_2P\|_2^2,\qquad
 \eta_6(B)=c_6(1+B)^{-6}.}
 \tag{PG19}
\]
The constant part has zero gradient; arbitrary affine terms are
still present on the left. No orthogonality under \(\rho\) was assumed.
Optimizing (PG18) over \(0<r\le1\) also proves
\[
 \int|\nabla P|^2\rho\,dU\ge\Lambda_6(B)\|\Pi_2P\|_2^2,\qquad
 \Lambda_6(B)=
 \begin{cases}c_6e^{-B},&0\le B\le6,\\
 c_6(6/(eB))^6,&B\ge6.
 \end{cases}
 \tag{PG20}
\]
For a finite vector of such polynomials, summing the proved
inequality component by component proves the same result.

\subsection*{5. The exponent six is necessary}

The full polynomial \(P_*=(1-c)^2=1-2c+c^2\) has
\(\|\Pi_2P_*\|_2^2=1/16\). Its affine term is essential to its
third-order gradient zero at \(I\). For \(B\ge1\), retain
\[
 t(U)=\sqrt{8(1-c(U))},\quad
 f_B(U)=\sqrt{B^{-2}+t(U)^2},\quad
 \rho_B(U)=Z_B^{-1}e^{-Bf_B(U)},\quad
 Z_B=\int e^{-Bf_B(U)}dU .
 \tag{PG21}
\]
These are smooth positive densities because \(f_B\) is the square
root of a strictly positive smooth function. Direct differentiation
in the radius-two metric gives
\[
 |\nabla t|^2=1-t^2/16,\qquad
 |\nabla f_B|^2=
 \frac{t^2(1-t^2/16)}{B^{-2}+t^2}\le1,\qquad
 |\nabla P_*|^2=\frac{t^6}{256}-\frac{t^8}{4096}.
 \tag{PG22}
\]
The first equality is used away from \(I\) and continued in the
second equality; \(t\) itself need not be smooth at \(I\).
Thus \(|\nabla\log\rho_B|\le B\). The exact radial Haar measure is
\[
 dU=\frac{t^2}{4\pi}\sqrt{1-t^2/16}\,dt,\quad0\le t\le4,\qquad
 I_k=\int_0^\infty u^k e^{-\sqrt{1+u^2}}\,du .
 \tag{PG23}
\]
This follows from \(t=4\sin(\theta/2)\) and (PG4).
Substituting \(u=Bt\), the denominator \(4\pi B^3Z_B\) is
\(\int_0^{4B}u^2\sqrt{1-u^2/(16B^2)}
e^{-\sqrt{1+u^2}}du\), which tends to \(I_2>0\).
For the gradient numerator the same substitution and (PG22) give
\[
 B^6\int|\nabla P_*|^2\rho_BdU
 =\frac{\displaystyle\int_0^{4B}
  \left(\frac{u^8}{256}-\frac{u^{10}}{4096B^2}\right)
       \sqrt{1-\frac{u^2}{16B^2}}e^{-\sqrt{1+u^2}}du}
 {\displaystyle\int_0^{4B}u^2
       \sqrt{1-\frac{u^2}{16B^2}}e^{-\sqrt{1+u^2}}du}
 \longrightarrow \frac{I_8}{256I_2}>0 .
 \tag{PG24}
\]
Dominated convergence is justified by \(u^k e^{-u}\), for each
displayed power, after extending each integrand by zero past \(4B\).
The numerator can equivalently be written as
\(u^8(1-u^2/(16B^2))^{3/2}e^{-\sqrt{1+u^2}}/256\).
Consequently no positive constant times \((1+B)^{-k}\|\Pi_2P\|_2^2\)
can hold uniformly for \(k<6\). This proves optimality of the
exponent, not optimality of the numerical constant (PG16).

\subsection*{6. Complete original lattice and score receiving map}

Let \(m=2L\), \(n=m+1\), \(h=\pi/(2n)\), with \(L\ge2\).
Vertices have indices \(0\le i,k,l\le m\) and physical positions
\(o+a(i-L,k-L,l-L)\). All positively oriented nearest-neighbour
edges and every elementary face of the open cube are retained.
The spanning tree consists of every direction-one edge, every
direction-two edge at first coordinate zero and every direction-three
edge at first two coordinates zero. Root paths run in directions
three, two, one from \((0,0,0)\). With ordered path transport \(t_s\),
the exact rooted word on a chord \(e=(s,t)\) is \(Z_e=t_sU_et_t^{-1}\).
The two chord sets and every original row coefficient are
\[
 \begin{gathered}
 \mathcal C_2=\{(i,k,l;2):1\le i\le m,\ 0\le k<m,\ 0\le l\le m\},\\
 \mathcal C_3=\{(i,k,l;3):0\le i,k\le m,\ (i,k)\ne(0,0),\
                                      0\le l<m\},\\
 v_0(t)=n^{-1/2},\quad
 v_1(t)=\sqrt{2/n}\cos\frac{\pi(t+1/2)}n,\quad
 w_1(t)=-\sqrt{2/n}\sin\frac{\pi(t+1)}n,\\
 a_{(i,k,l;2)}=-v_1(i)w_1(k)v_0(l)/\sqrt2,\quad
 a_{(i,k,l;3)}=0,\\
 b_{(i,k,l;2)}=v_0(i)w_1(k)v_1(l)/\sqrt2,\quad
 b_{(i,k,l;3)}=-v_0(i)v_1(k)w_1(l)/\sqrt2,\\
 u_1=\sum_{e\in\mathcal C_2\cup\mathcal C_3}a_eq(Z_e),\quad
 u_3=\sum_{e\in\mathcal C_2\cup\mathcal C_3}b_eq(Z_e),\\
 S=u_1\cdot u_3,\quad S_{\mathcal R}=S\circ\mathcal R,\quad
 \sigma_*=\sqrt8\sin h,\quad\beta_0=\frac{\sigma_*}{2g^2},\quad
 F=p-r=\beta_0(S-S_{\mathcal R}).
 \end{gathered}\tag{PG25}
\]
The symbol \(\mathcal R\) is the original direction-one graph
reflection, including its reflected root and every inverse edge
orientation. The polynomial coefficient \(B_{\rm aff}\) of Section 1 is
distinct from the score bound \(B\) and all boundary link indices.

The complete Hamiltonian and actual positive unit vacuum are
\[
 \begin{gathered}
 H=\kappa H_0+b_{\rm mag}\sum_{\text{all faces }p}
                       (2-\operatorname{tr}U_p),\quad
 H_0=-\sum_{e,\alpha}X_{e,\alpha}^2,\quad H\psi=E_0\psi,\quad
 d\mu=\psi^2dU,\\
 \kappa=\frac{2g^2}{a},\quad b_{\rm mag}=\frac1{2g^2a},\quad
 \xi=\frac1{4g^4},\quad
 C_F=\langle F\psi,(H-E_0)F\psi\rangle
                      =\kappa\mu(|\nabla F|^2),\quad G=\mu(F^2).
 \end{gathered}\tag{PG26}
\]
The constant \(2b_{\rm mag}M\), \(M=3m^2(m+1)\), remains in the
Hamiltonian and vacuum equation. The displayed energy identity follows
by expanding \(H(F\psi)\), substituting \(H\psi=E_0\psi\), and
integrating the two derivative cross terms by parts on the compact
physical-link product.

The exact score statement read in the retained original TeX is
\[
 |\nabla_e\log\psi^2|\le4r_e\xi ,
 \tag{PG27}
\]
where \(r_e\) is the actual number of faces incident to the physical
edge. Its proof, including every mixed derivative, is in
\href{https://github.com/KokunoYumeto/yang-mills-interacting-workbench/blob/485b61423c1f4e1805deb453c1b6b342b27842b6/yang-mills/continuations/20260930-s6-ns-moment-map-bridge/sources/spatial/spatial_continuum.tex#L1415}{equations (77)–(79) of the original TeX}
and is repeated here to specify precisely what is used.
Writing \(u=\log\psi\), the vacuum equation implies
\(\Delta u+|\nabla u|^2=(V-E_0)/\kappa\).
The product Bochner formula gives
\[
 (\Delta+2\nabla u\cdot\nabla)|\nabla_eu|^2
 =2\sum_f|\nabla_f\nabla_eu|_{\rm HS}^2+
       |\nabla_eu|^2+2\xi\langle\nabla_eu,\nabla_eW\rangle ,
 \qquad W=\sum_p(2-\operatorname{tr}U_p).
\]
The coefficient one is twice the Ricci curvature \(1/2\) in the
radius-two metric. An incident face word is \(AUB\) or \(AU^{-1}B\);
bi-invariance reduces its trace gradient to that of \(2c\), whose
norm is at most one. Hence \(|\nabla_eW|\le r_e\). At a maximum
of \(|\nabla_eu|^2\), the Bochner identity gives
\(0\ge|\nabla_eu|^2-2\xi r_e|\nabla_eu|\).
This proves (PG27).

The tree-coordinate map sends physical product Haar to product Haar
in tree coordinates and all chords: solve successively
\(U_{(s,t)}=t_s^{-1}t_t\) for tree edges, and
\(U_e=t_s^{-1}Z_et_t\) for chords, using left and right Haar
invariance at each step. Gauge invariance of the positive vacuum
follows from uniqueness of the ground state and its positivity, so its
tree-coordinate density is independent of tree coordinates.
For a fixed chord and fixed tree coordinates, varying \(U_e\) varies
only \(Z_e=t_sU_et_t^{-1}\). Conjugation by \(t_s\) rotates the
three left derivative components orthogonally. Consequently the
physical squared derivative norm equals the chord squared derivative
norm, and the actual one-chord conditional score is bounded by
(PG27). The denominator in a conditional density is constant in that
chord variable. Integrating the density over other chords also preserves
the score bound: differentiate the integral and apply
\(|\nabla\rho|\le B\rho\). These facts justify both conditional and
marginal uses below without asserting independence.

\subsection*{7. Exact quadratic harmonic at every direction-two bulk chord}

Take
\[
 e=(i,k,l;2),\quad1\le i\le m-1,\quad0\le k<m,\quad0\le l\le m,
 \qquad e_\partial(e)=(m,k,l;2).
 \tag{PG28}
\]
Write \(K=Z_e\), \(H_\partial=Z_{e_\partial(e)}\). Boundary links
are denoted by \(e_\partial\), while \(b_e\) always denotes the
original row coefficient in (PG25).

For completeness define the ordered boundary comb
\[
 A_{k,l}=
 \left(\prod_{t=0}^{l-1}Z_{(m,0,t;3)}\right)
 \left(\prod_{s=0}^{k-1}Z_{(m,s,l;2)}\right),\qquad A_{0,0}=I.
 \tag{PG29}
\]
Every product is in increasing displayed order, and an empty product
is identity. After evaluating the original root paths in tree gauge,
their reflected transports are \(A_{k,l}\), independently of the first
coordinate. Direction-one paths have identity transport in that gauge.
The unique reflected source containing the target chord \(K\) is
\((m-i,k,l;2)\), with the same edge orientation. Since
\(A_{k+1,l}=A_{k,l}H_\partial\), its entire reflected word is
\[
 Z^{\mathcal R}_{(m-i,k,l;2)}
       =A_{k,l}K H_\partial^{-1}A_{k,l}^{-1}.
 \tag{PG30}
\]
No other reflected target is \(K\), and \(K\) occurs in no boundary
comb because \(i<m\). The comb \(A_{k,l}\) is independent of
\(H_\partial\) as well: its direction-two factors end at \(k-1\).
The adjoint action of \(A_{k,l}\) on \(q\) is orthogonal, since
\(-2\operatorname{tr}(T_\alpha T_\beta)=\delta_{\alpha\beta}\).
It preserves both \(c\) and \(|q|^2\).

Let \(U_1,U_3\) be the complete original row sums excluding \(e\),
and \(V_1,V_3\) the complete reflected row sums excluding
\((m-i,k,l;2)\), respectively. All four depend on the exterior and
are independent of \(K\). Put \(Q=q(K)\),
\(Q'=\operatorname{Ad}_{A_{k,l}}q(KH_\partial^{-1})\).
The exact coefficient reflection is
\(a_{(m-i,k,l;2)}=-a_e\), \(b_{(m-i,k,l;2)}=b_e\), by
\(v_1(m-i)=-v_1(i)\). The full expansions, including constants, are
\[
 \begin{aligned}
 S={}&U_1\cdot U_3+(b_eU_1+a_eU_3)\cdot Q
                              +a_eb_e|Q|^2,\\
 S_{\mathcal R}={}&V_1\cdot V_3+
                    (b_eV_1-a_eV_3)\cdot Q'-a_eb_e|Q'|^2,\\
 F={}&\beta_0\bigl[U_1\cdot U_3-V_1\cdot V_3
       +(b_eU_1+a_eU_3)\cdot Q
       -(b_eV_1-a_eV_3)\cdot Q'
       +a_eb_e(|Q|^2+|Q'|^2)\bigr].
 \end{aligned}\tag{PG31}
\]
Thus, for each exterior, \(F\) is in the complete space treated by
(PG19). Its remainder is affine in all four quaternion coordinates
of \(K\). Derivatives in the physical chord \(e\) differentiate
exactly this polynomial through the isometry already proved.

Set \(\chi_1(U)=4c(U)^2-1\), a degree-two harmonic of Haar squared
norm one. Since \(|q(U)|^2=3-\chi_1(U)\),
\[
 \Pi_2^{(e)}F=-\beta_0a_eb_e
          [\chi_1(K)+\chi_1(KH_\partial^{-1})],\qquad
 \|\Pi_2^{(e)}F\|_{L^2(dK)}^2
 =\frac43\beta_0^2a_e^2b_e^2[1+2c(H_\partial)^2].
 \tag{PG32}
\]
Indeed, multiplication by \(H_\partial^{-1}\) is an orthogonal
linear map of quaternion coordinates. If its scalar coordinate is
\(\ell\cdot z\), then \(|\ell|=1\) and
\(\ell\cdot(1,0,0,0)=c(H_\partial)\).
The fourth-moment identity preceding (PG4) gives
\(\int z_0^2(\ell\cdot z)^2=(1+2c(H_\partial)^2)/24\).
Expanding the two factors \(4z_0^2-1\) and
\(4(\ell\cdot z)^2-1\) yields their inner product
\((4c(H_\partial)^2-1)/3\).
Adding their two unit squared norms proves (PG32), with every
constant term of the original polynomial still retained in (PG31).

\subsection*{8. Distinct physical energies and all boundary face counts}

For (PG28), the two direction-\((1,2)\) faces always exist, because
\(0<i<m\). Direction-\((2,3)\) faces exist below and above exactly
when \(l>0\) and \(l<m\), respectively. For \(e_\partial(e)\)
only one direction-\((1,2)\) face exists. Therefore
\[
 r_e=2+\mathbf1_{l>0}+\mathbf1_{l<m},\qquad
 r_{e_\partial(e)}=1+\mathbf1_{l>0}+\mathbf1_{l<m},\qquad
 B_l=\begin{cases}12\xi,&l=0,m,\\16\xi,&0<l<m.\end{cases}
 \tag{PG33}
\]
There is no extra \(k\)-face penalty: the edge points in direction
two, so only the two transverse coordinates determine incidence.

Apply (PG19) to the actual \(K\)-conditional density and full
polynomial (PG31), then integrate the exterior. Its \(\Pi_2\) norm
is exactly (PG32). Since \(H_\partial\) is a different chord, the
exterior marginal of its squared scalar coordinate is precisely
the full \(\mu\) marginal. Distinct \(e\)'s are distinct physical
derivative summands in (PG26); summing positive quantities requires
no independence. Hence
\[
 \boxed{\displaystyle
 C_F\ge\frac{4\kappa\beta_0^2}{3}
 \sum_{\substack{1\le i\le m-1\\0\le k<m\\0\le l\le m}}
 a_{(i,k,l;2)}^2b_{(i,k,l;2)}^2\,\eta_6(B_l)
     [1+2\mu(c(Z_{(m,k,l;2)})^2)].}
 \tag{PG34}
\]
This relates the original energy directly to its actual conditional
quadratic harmonic data. Every other physical derivative remains a
nonnegative summand of the original energy. Replacing \(\eta_6\)
by \(\Lambda_6\) throughout (PG34) is also valid, by (PG20).

The exact coefficient and row sums are
\[
 \begin{gathered}
 a_{(i,k,l;2)}^2b_{(i,k,l;2)}^2
 =\frac4{n^6}\cos^2\frac{\pi(i+1/2)}n
       \cos^2\frac{\pi(l+1/2)}n\sin^4\frac{\pi(k+1)}n,\\
 J_n=\frac n2-2\cos^2h,\qquad
 \sum_{i=1}^{m-1}\cos^2\frac{\pi(i+1/2)}n=J_n,\qquad
 \sum_{k=0}^{m-1}\sin^4\frac{\pi(k+1)}n=\frac{3n}{8},\\
 \sum_{l\in\{0,m\}}\cos^2\frac{\pi(l+1/2)}n=2\cos^2h,\qquad
 \sum_{l=1}^{m-1}\cos^2\frac{\pi(l+1/2)}n=J_n .
 \end{gathered}\tag{PG35}
\]
For the cosine sums, expand \(\cos^2x=(1+\cos2x)/2\) and sum
the complete \(n\)-term geometric progression; its oscillatory sum
is zero. Remove exactly the two endpoint terms \(\cos^2h\).
For the sine sum, use
\(\sin^4x=(3-4\cos2x+\cos4x)/8\).
On the nonzero residues \(1,\ldots,n-1\), each of the two cosine
sums is \(-1\). This uses \(n\ge5\), so neither frequency is zero
modulo \(n\). The result is
\([3(n-1)+4-1]/8=3n/8\).
The original family includes \(2m(m-1)\) links with \(r_e=3\)
and \(m(m-1)^2\) links with \(r_e=4\); their sum is
\(m(m-1)(m+1)\), exactly the cardinality of (PG28).

Using only \(1+2\mu c^2\ge1\), (PG34) becomes the fully evaluated
bound
\[
 C_F\ge\frac{2\kappa\beta_0^2J_n}{n^5}
       \left[2\cos^2h\,\eta_6(12\xi)+J_n\eta_6(16\xi)\right].
 \tag{PG36}
\]
Before this last inequality, the exact receiving expression with its
remaining boundary expectations is
\[
 \frac{16\kappa\beta_0^2J_n}{3n^6}
 \sum_{k=0}^{m-1}\sin^4\frac{\pi(k+1)}n
 \sum_{l=0}^{m}\cos^2\frac{\pi(l+1/2)}n\,\eta_6(B_l)
             [1+2\mu(c(Z_{(m,k,l;2)})^2)] .
 \tag{PG37}
\]
It is the right side of (PG34), not an assumed independent product.

\subsection*{9. A further calculation of the boundary-coordinate contribution}

The unresolved expectations in (PG37) can themselves be bounded
polynomially from the proved score estimate. For any centre \(z\),
radial symmetry and quaternion multiplication give exactly
\[
 \frac1{v(r)}\int_{d(z,U)\le r}c(U)^2dU
 =c(z)^2t_r+\frac{1-c(z)^2}{3}(1-t_r),\qquad
 t_r=\frac{\int_0^{r/2}\sin^2\theta\cos^2\theta\,d\theta}
            {\int_0^{r/2}\sin^2\theta\,d\theta}.
 \tag{PG38}
\]
For \(r\le1\), \(t_r\ge\cos^2(1/2)>1/4\).
Thus its minimum over \(z\) is \((1-t_r)/3\). By (PG9),
\[
 \frac{1-t_r}{3}
 =\frac{I_0}{3J_0}
 \ge\frac{\alpha_0^4r^2}{20}.
 \tag{PG39}
\]
Averaging centres exactly as in (PG18) proves
\(\int c^2\rho\ge\alpha_0^4 e^{-Br}r^2/20\).
For \(B\le2\), choose \(r=1\); for \(B\ge2\), choose \(r=2/B\).
In the first range, the minimum of
\(e^{-B}(1+B)^2\) over \([0,2]\) is one, as differentiation
shows. In the second range \((1+B)/B\ge1\).
Consequently both ranges give
\[
 \int c^2\rho\,dU\ge\frac{\eta_q(B)}4,\qquad
 \eta_q(B)=c_q(1+B)^{-2},\qquad
 c_q=\frac4{5e^2}(23/24)^4 .
 \tag{PG40}
\]
For the first range the chosen constant is valid because
\(4/e^2<1\). This proof explicitly controls the original scalar
coordinate, not a colour-vector replacement.

The actual boundary marginal has score \(8\xi\) at \(l=0,m\)
and \(12\xi\) at \(0<l<m\), by (PG27), (PG33) and the marginal
score argument following (PG27). Inserting (PG40) into (PG37),
then evaluating the same exact row sums, gives the strengthening
\[
 \boxed{\displaystyle
 C_F\ge {\cal C}(L,a,g):=
 \frac{2\kappa\beta_0^2J_n}{n^5}
 \left[
 2\cos^2h\,\eta_6(12\xi)\left(1+\frac{\eta_q(8\xi)}2\right)
 +J_n\eta_6(16\xi)\left(1+\frac{\eta_q(12\xi)}2\right)
 \right].}
 \tag{PG41}
\]
This retains both boundary classes and every displayed positive
contribution. The version with \(\Lambda_6\) in place of \(\eta_6\)
is also proved. The remaining physical derivatives and the stronger
actual expectations (PG37) are additional nonnegative contributions,
not assumed equalities in (PG41).

\subsection*{10. The unchanged original joint path and the endpoint}

Use the original path, with integer \(j\ge2\):
\[
 L_j=j^4,\quad a_j=\frac1{100j},\quad
 g_j^2=\frac{\kappa_*}{200j},\quad s_j=\frac{\kappa_*}{j},\quad
 n_j=2j^4+1,\quad
 \kappa_j=\kappa_*,\quad \xi_j=\frac{10000j^2}{\kappa_*^2},
 \quad\kappa_*>0.
 \tag{PG42}
\]
In particular
\(j^3\beta_{0,j}\to50\sqrt2\pi/\kappa_*\),
\(J_{n_j}/n_j\to1/2\) and \(n_j/j^4\to2\).
Let \({\cal C}_{\partial,j}\) and \({\cal C}_{{\rm int},j}\)
be respectively the first and second summands on the right of
(PG41), including their respective \(\eta_q\) factors. Direct
substitution into their full formulas gives the positive limits
\[
 \begin{aligned}
 j^{34}{\cal C}_{\partial,j}
 &\longrightarrow
 \frac{625\pi^2}{\kappa_*}\,c_6
               \left(\frac{\kappa_*^2}{120000}\right)^6,\\
 j^{30}{\cal C}_{{\rm int},j}
 &\longrightarrow
 \frac{625\pi^2}{2\kappa_*}\,c_6
               \left(\frac{\kappa_*^2}{160000}\right)^6,\\
 j^{30}{\cal C}(L_j,a_j,g_j)
 &\longrightarrow
 \frac{625\pi^2}{2\kappa_*}\,c_6
               \left(\frac{\kappa_*^2}{160000}\right)^6.
 \end{aligned}\tag{PG43}
\]
For example, the interior prefactor
\(2\kappa_*\beta_{0,j}^2J_{n_j}^2/n_j^5\) times \(j^{18}\)
tends to \(625\pi^2/(2\kappa_*)\), while
\(j^{12}\eta_6(16\xi_j)\to
c_6(\kappa_*^2/160000)^6\).
Both \(\eta_q\) factors tend to zero. This proves every limit
in (PG43) from the original coefficients, without selecting a
different coupling. For the \(\Lambda_6\) version, replace the
factor \(c_6\) in both limits by \(c_6(6/e)^6\).

The previous single-link energy estimate (PC49), proved in
\href{https://github.com/KokunoYumeto/yang-mills-interacting-workbench/blob/main/yang-mills/continuations/20261011-central-signs-shrinking-window/POLYNOMIAL_CONDITIONAL_BOUNDS.md}{the complete retained PC note},
was of order \(j^{-46}\). The complete gradient inequality and
the exact bulk sum improve this to the proved lower bound
\(C_{F,j}\ge{\cal C}(L_j,a_j,g_j)\) with order \(j^{-30}\).
These are asymptotics of the stated bounds, not equalities for the
actual energy.

Finally preserve the original moments
\[
 Q_4=\mu(S^2),\quad Q_4^{\mathcal R}=\mu(SS_{\mathcal R}),\quad
 K_1=\mu(|\nabla S|^2),\quad
 K_{\mathcal R}=\mu(\nabla S\cdot\nabla S_{\mathcal R}).
 \tag{PG44}
\]
Reflection invariance of the actual vacuum and kinetic form gives
\(G=2\beta_0^2(Q_4-Q_4^{\mathcal R})\) and
\(C_F=2\kappa\beta_0^2(K_1-K_{\mathcal R})\), by expanding
the complete original \(F\) of (PG25). Therefore the requested
raw expression satisfies the exact receiving inequality
\[
 \begin{aligned}
 {\cal E}_j(s_j)
 &:=
 2\beta_{0,j}^2(Q_{4,j}-Q_{4,j}^{\mathcal R})
 -\frac{2\kappa_*\beta_{0,j}^2}{s_j}
                         (K_{1,j}-K_{\mathcal R,j})\\
 &=G_j-\frac{C_{F,j}}{s_j}
 \ \le\ G_j-\frac{j}{\kappa_*}{\cal C}(L_j,a_j,g_j).
 \end{aligned}\tag{PG45}
\]
The subtracted positive term in the last line has order \(j^{-29}\)
and exact leading coefficient
\[
 \frac{625\pi^2}{2\kappa_*^2}\,c_6
                 \left(\frac{\kappa_*^2}{160000}\right)^6.
 \tag{PG46}
\]
A separate lower bound on \(G_j\) cannot be inserted into this upper
bound to infer its sign. Neither (PG34) nor (PG41) determines a
quotient \(C_F/G\), and neither identifies spectral mass in
\((0,s_j]\). What is proved is a uniform complete-polynomial
gradient inequality with optimal exponent, its exact original-word
receiving map, the entire bulk coefficient sum, a further bound on
its boundary-coordinate remainder, and the endpoint inequality
(PG45). The upper control or exact joint-path evaluation of \(G_j\)
and the distribution of the actual spectral measure remain unresolved.

The source-reading ledger records the original TeX read and the
existing disk-index routing used before the new derivation. No
unread source inequality or claim of external novelty enters this proof.

\subsection*{11. Preserve the density floor and its full Casimir factor}

The preceding \(\eta_6\) estimates remain valid. A further improvement
keeps the full Haar Dirichlet energy in the density-floor branch.
For a unit density with score bound \(B\), the diameter \(2\pi\)
gives \(\rho\ge e^{-2\pi B}\). Indeed its maximum is at least one,
and the logarithmic oscillation is at most \(2\pi B\).
For the complete polynomial of (PG2), the exact Haar energy is
\[
 \int|\nabla P|^2dU
   =\frac34\|\Pi_1P\|_2^2+2\|\Pi_2P\|_2^2 .
 \tag{PG47}
\]
To verify the two eigenvalues, the Euclidean polar-coordinate
Laplacian in dimension four sends \(r^kY(\omega)\) to
\(r^{k-2}[k(k+2)Y+\Delta_{S^3}Y]\). Harmonicity gives the
radius-one eigenvalue \(k(k+2)\); the original radius-two metric
divides it by four. Integration by parts and harmonic
orthogonality give (PG47), including the affine contribution.
Combining it with (PG20) proves the stronger complete-polynomial
inequality
\[
 \boxed{\int|\nabla P|^2\rho\,dU
        \ge\Psi_6(B)\|\Pi_2P\|_2^2,\qquad
 \Psi_6(B)=\max\{2e^{-2\pi B},\Lambda_6(B)\}.}
 \tag{PG48}
\]
In particular \(\Psi_6(0)=2\). The factor two in its first branch
cannot be replaced by one when recording the full available bound.
Since \(\Lambda_6\ge\eta_6\), all of (PG34), (PG36), (PG37),
(PG41) and (PG45) hold with \(\eta_6\) replaced by \(\Psi_6\),
and the original displayed versions are preserved.

For clarity, the full evaluations with the original \(\eta_q\)
retained are
\[
 \begin{aligned}
 C_F&\ge\frac{4\kappa\beta_0^2}{3}
       \sum_ea_e^2b_e^2\Psi_6(B_l)
                       [1+2\mu(c(H_\partial)^2)]\\
 &\ge\frac{2\kappa\beta_0^2J_n}{n^5}
 \left[
 2\cos^2h\,\Psi_6(12\xi)(1+\tfrac12\eta_q(8\xi))
 +J_n\Psi_6(16\xi)(1+\tfrac12\eta_q(12\xi))
 \right].
 \end{aligned}\tag{PG49}
\]
The sum is over exactly (PG28), with \(H_\partial=Z_{(m,k,l;2)}\)
and the exact face classes of (PG33). Thus the stronger factor is
propagated through the actual harmonic-data sum as well as its
fully evaluated version.

\subsection*{12. A stronger pure-linear marginal bound, with its proof}

The following constant and optimized factor were independently
proved in
\href{https://github.com/KokunoYumeto/yang-mills-interacting-workbench/blob/main/yang-mills/continuations/20261011-central-signs-shrinking-window/POLYNOMIAL_SCORE_AND_ORIGINAL_ENDPOINT.md}{the complete PS derivation, PS17–PS18}.
The complete argument used here is repeated to keep every comparison
explicit. For a physical cap of radius \(r\le1\), put
\(\delta=1-\cos(r/2)\) and \(u=1-\cos\theta\). Its exact radial
probability measure is
\[
 d\nu_\delta(u)=
 \frac{\sqrt{u(2-u)}\,du}{\int_0^\delta\sqrt{v(2-v)}\,dv}
 \ge\frac{15}{16}\frac{3u^{1/2}}{2\delta^{3/2}}\,du,
 \qquad
 \frac{47r^2}{384}\le\delta\le\frac{r^2}{8}\le\frac18 .
 \tag{PG50}
\]
The cosine bounds follow from its alternating series. For the
measure inequality, bound the numerator factor \(\sqrt{2-u}\)
below by \(\sqrt{2-\delta}\), and its denominator factor above
by \(\sqrt2\). Their ratio is at least
\(\sqrt{15}/4\ge15/16\); integrating \(u^{1/2}\) gives the
remaining coefficient.

For a pure linear quaternion function \(\ell(z)=a\cdot z\), the
cap second-moment eigenvalues are \(\operatorname{Av}\cos^2\theta\)
in the central direction and \(\operatorname{Av}\sin^2\theta/3\)
in its orthogonal complement. The latter is smaller for
\(\theta\le1/2\), because \(\cos\theta\ge7/8\).
Equation (PG50) gives \(\operatorname{Av}u\ge9\delta/16\),
and \(u(2-u)\ge15u/8\). Since the full Haar norm is
\(|a|^2/4\), the cap average is at least
\[
 \operatorname{Av}|\ell|^2
 \ge C_1r^2\|\ell\|_2^2,\qquad C_1=\frac{705}{4096}.
 \tag{PG51}
\]
The exact product is
\((4/3)(15/8)(9/16)(47/384)=705/4096\).
The argument is valid for complex coefficients by the Hermitian
second-moment form.

Define \(r_k(B)=1\) for \(B\le k\), and \(r_k(B)=k/B\)
for \(B>k\). The cap-centre averaging proof (PG18), applied
to \(|\ell|^2\), and the Haar density floor give
\[
 \begin{gathered}
 \Theta_1(B)=\max\{e^{-2\pi B},
            C_1e^{-B r_2(B)}r_2(B)^2\},\\
 \int|\ell|^2\rho\,dU\ge\Theta_1(B)\|\ell\|_2^2,\qquad
 \mu(c(H_\partial)^2)\ge\tfrac14\Theta_1(B_\partial),\qquad
 \mu(|q(H_\partial)|^2)\ge3\Theta_1(B_\partial).
 \end{gathered}\tag{PG52}
\]
The last two assertions apply the pure-linear result to the scalar
coordinate and to each of the three coordinates \(2x_\alpha\);
they use the actual boundary marginal and its score
\(B_\partial=8\xi\) at \(l=0,m\), \(12\xi\) otherwise.
They are not assertions about an arbitrary affine polynomial.
The new factor is at least \(\eta_q\): the exact inequality
\(C_1\ge\alpha_0^4/5\) and optimization over \(r\le1\)
dominate the cap bound used in (PG39)–(PG40).

\subsection*{13. The complete even polynomial and both evaluated bulk sums}

Define \(D=S+S_{\mathcal R}\), with the same full original rows.
Adding the first two lines of (PG31), rather than subtracting
them, gives its exact conditional polynomial
\[
 \begin{aligned}
 D={}&U_1\cdot U_3+V_1\cdot V_3
 +(b_eU_1+a_eU_3)\cdot Q
 +(b_eV_1-a_eV_3)\cdot Q'\\
 &\hspace{20mm}+a_eb_e(|Q|^2-|Q'|^2),\\
 \Pi_2^{(e)}D={}&-a_eb_e[\chi_1(K)-\chi_1(KH_\partial^{-1})],\\
 \|\Pi_2^{(e)}D\|_{L^2(dK)}^2
 ={}&a_e^2b_e^2\left[2-\frac23(4c(H_\partial)^2-1)\right]
   =\frac23a_e^2b_e^2|q(H_\partial)|^2 .
 \end{aligned}\tag{PG53}
\]
The fourth-moment calculation in (PG32) proves the last two
equalities. All constants and affine terms remain in the first
two lines. Applying (PG48) to this complete polynomial at each
bulk chord, then integrating its actual exterior, gives
\[
 \begin{aligned}
 \mu\left(\sum_{e\ {\rm in}\ (PG28)}|\nabla_eD|^2\right)
 &\ge\frac23\sum_ea_e^2b_e^2\Psi_6(B_l)
                            \mu(|q(H_\partial)|^2),\\
 \mu\left(\sum_{e\ {\rm in}\ (PG28)}|\nabla_eF|^2\right)
 &\ge\frac{4\beta_0^2}{3}
       \sum_ea_e^2b_e^2\Psi_6(B_l)
                            [1+2\mu(c(H_\partial)^2)] .
 \end{aligned}\tag{PG54}
\]
Using (PG52) and every coefficient sum in (PG35) evaluates these
as follows:
\[
 \boxed{\begin{aligned}
 A_D={}&\frac{3J_n}{n^5}
   [2\cos^2h\,\Psi_6(12\xi)\Theta_1(8\xi)
                 +J_n\Psi_6(16\xi)\Theta_1(12\xi)],\\
 A_F={}&\frac{2J_n}{n^5}
   [2\cos^2h\,\Psi_6(12\xi)(1+\tfrac12\Theta_1(8\xi))
                 +J_n\Psi_6(16\xi)(1+\tfrac12\Theta_1(12\xi))],\\
 \mu\left(\sum_{e\ {\rm in}\ (PG28)}|\nabla_eD|^2\right)&\ge A_D,
 \qquad
 \mu\left(\sum_{e\ {\rm in}\ (PG28)}|\nabla_eF|^2\right)
                                                    \ge\beta_0^2A_F .
 \end{aligned}}\tag{PG55}
\]
For the even prefactor, \((2/3)\mu|q|^2\ge2\Theta_1\);
multiplying the coefficient sum \(3J_n/(2n^5)\) yields
\(3J_n/n^5\). For the odd prefactor the corresponding product
is \((4/3)\,3J_n/(2n^5)=2J_n/n^5\).
This proves the constants without a comparison of independent
mass and energy lower bounds.

\subsection*{14. Add the disjoint boundary derivatives with their complete vector bound}

The separately proved boundary estimate is
\href{https://github.com/KokunoYumeto/yang-mills-interacting-workbench/blob/main/yang-mills/continuations/20261011-central-signs-shrinking-window/POLYNOMIAL_SCORE_AND_ORIGINAL_ENDPOINT.md}{PS19–PS26 and PS40–PS59}.
Its particular vector argument and exact original receiving map are
repeated here. For the complete vector polynomial
\[
 {\cal P}_\alpha(U)=a\,c(U)q_\alpha(U)+R_\alpha(U),
 \qquad\alpha=1,2,3,
 \tag{PG56}
\]
each \(R_\alpha\) is an arbitrary affine function of all four
quaternion coordinates. For a unit cap centre \(z\in S^3\), set
\(M_\alpha=e_0e_\alpha^{\mathsf T}+e_\alpha e_0^{\mathsf T}\)
and \(b_\alpha=2(I-zz^{\mathsf T})M_\alpha z\).
Writing \(x=t z+\sqrt{1-t^2}\,\omega\), the angular vector
block of the full polynomial is
\(\sqrt{1-t^2}(v_\alpha+a\,t b_\alpha)\cdot\omega\).
Its other scalar and tensor blocks retain their nonnegative
squared norms by angular orthogonality. Put \(u=1-t\).
In the vector block,
\((1-t^2)/3\ge5u/8\). Using (PG50), the radial matrix for
the free coefficient and the fixed slope, after scaling \(u/\delta\),
has the exact entries \(3/5,3/7,1/3\). Its Schur complement is
\[
 \frac13-\frac{(3/7)^2}{3/5}=\frac4{147}.
 \tag{PG57}
\]
Thus completing the square gives the cap lower bound
\((15/16)(5/8)(4/147)\delta^3|a|^2|b_\alpha|^2
=(25/1568)\delta^3|a|^2|b_\alpha|^2\).
For a spatial rotation making \(z=(c,s,0,0)\), the three cross
vectors have squared lengths
\(4(c^2-s^2)^2,4c^2,4c^2\). Therefore
\[
 \sum_\alpha|b_\alpha|^2
 =3+16(s^2-\tfrac34)^2\ge3,\qquad
 C_6^{\rm vec}=\frac{75}{784}\left(\frac{47}{384}\right)^3 .
 \tag{PG58}
\]
The same identity holds before rotation, since rotating the three
spatial axes rotates the family \(M_\alpha\) orthogonally.
The cap average of the full sum of squares is consequently
at least \((|a|^2/2)C_6^{\rm vec}r^6\). Its full-Haar quadratic
norm is \(|a|^2/2\), since
\(\int\sum_\alpha|cq_\alpha|^2=4(1/4-1/8)=1/2\);
these harmonics are orthogonal to all the affine remainders.
The cap averaging and the density floor hence give
\[
 \begin{gathered}
 \Theta_6^{\rm vec}(B)
 =\max\{e^{-2\pi B},
      C_6^{\rm vec}e^{-B r_6(B)}r_6(B)^6\},\\
 \int\sum_\alpha|{\cal P}_\alpha|^2\rho\,dU
                       \ge\frac{|a|^2}{2}\Theta_6^{\rm vec}(B).
 \end{gathered}\tag{PG59}
\]
This is exactly the PS function \(\Theta_6\); the superscript
distinguishes it from the stronger complete-gradient factor
\(\Psi_6\), which applies to a different integrand.

For each boundary chord \(e_\partial=(m,k,l;2)\), condition on
the bulk chord \(e=(1,k,l;2)\), with \(0\le k<m\), \(0\le l\le m\).
This single selected bulk chord serves only as the conditional
variable for a boundary derivative. It is not an extra summand
in (PG55). Its exact row coefficient is
\[
 d_{k,l}=-4a_{(1,k,l;2)}b_{(1,k,l;2)}
 =\frac{8\cos(3h)\cos((2l+1)h)\sin^2(\pi(k+1)/n)}{n^3}.
 \tag{PG60}
\]
The zero at \(l=L\) and both signs remain in the formula.
The left boundary variation \(H_\partial(t)=e^{tT_\alpha}H_\partial\)
gives \(KH_\partial(t)^{-1}=KH_\partial^{-1}e^{-tT_\alpha}\).
Direct quaternion multiplication yields
\(\partial_t c(Ve^{-tT_\alpha})|_0=q_\alpha(V)/4\) and
\(\partial_t|q(Ve^{-tT_\alpha})|^2|_{t=0}=-2c(V)q_\alpha(V)\).
Differentiate the complete expansions (PG31) and (PG53).
Since \(A_{k,l}\) is independent of this boundary link, the
quadratic terms of the entire physical derivatives are
\[
 \begin{aligned}
 X_{e_\partial,\alpha}D
    &=2a_eb_e\,c(KH_\partial^{-1})
                    q_\alpha(KH_\partial^{-1})+R^D_\alpha(K),\\
 X_{e_\partial,\alpha}F
    &=-2\beta_0a_eb_e\,c(KH_\partial^{-1})
                    q_\alpha(KH_\partial^{-1})+R^F_\alpha(K).
 \end{aligned}\tag{PG61}
\]
Every derivative of every exterior row sum remains in
\(R^D_\alpha,R^F_\alpha\). These are affine in \(K\):
right multiplication is linear in its four coordinates, and the
only quadratic terms of the original expansions were displayed
explicitly. Thus no term of the actual derivative is set to zero.
Right translation \(K\mapsto KH_\partial^{-1}\) preserves its
conditional score bound \(B_l\), metric and Haar measure.
Applying (PG59) and integrating the actual exterior proves
\[
 \begin{gathered}
 \mu(|\nabla_{e_\partial}D|^2)
           \ge\frac{d_{k,l}^2}{8}\Theta_6^{\rm vec}(B_l),\qquad
 \mu(|\nabla_{e_\partial}F|^2)
           \ge\frac{\beta_0^2d_{k,l}^2}{8}
                                      \Theta_6^{\rm vec}(B_l),\\
 B_6=\frac{3\cos^2(3h)}{n^5}
       [2\cos^2h\,\Theta_6^{\rm vec}(12\xi)
                         +J_n\Theta_6^{\rm vec}(16\xi)] .
 \end{gathered}\tag{PG62}
\]
The last line is the exact sum of the first bound over all
\(m(m+1)\) distinct boundary chords. Substituting (PG60) gives
the common coefficient \(64(3n/8)\cos^2(3h)/(8n^6)\);
the endpoint and interior \(l\)-sums are exactly those in (PG35).

Every boundary chord here has first coordinate \(m\), whereas
every bulk derivative in (PG55) has first coordinate at most
\(m-1\). These physical derivative sets are disjoint. Their
conditional variables may overlap, but their derivative energies
do not. Adding the already integrated expectations therefore proves
\[
 \boxed{\mu(|\nabla D|^2)\ge B_6+A_D,\qquad
 C_F\ge\kappa\beta_0^2(B_6+A_F).}
 \tag{PG63}
\]
This addition does not add two bounds on the same derivative,
does not add mass bounds, and does not posit independence.

\subsection*{15. Every path contribution and both original endpoint inequalities}

All factors in (PG63) have explicit limits on (PG42). Define
\[
 \begin{array}{ll}
 P_3=c_6(\kappa_*^2/(20000e))^6,&
 P_4=c_6(3\kappa_*^2/(80000e))^6,\\
 T_{1,2}=C_1(\kappa_*^2/(40000e))^2,&
 T_{1,3}=C_1(\kappa_*^2/(60000e))^2,\\
 T_{6,3}=C_6^{\rm vec}(\kappa_*^2/(20000e))^6,&
 T_{6,4}=C_6^{\rm vec}(3\kappa_*^2/(80000e))^6 .
 \end{array}\tag{PG64}
\]
For each fixed \(\kappa_*>0\), eventually all score arguments
exceed the respective optimization breakpoints. Their polynomial
branches then dominate the exponential branches, since
\(j^k e^{-b j^2}\to0\) for every \(b>0\) and fixed \(k\);
this follows by taking logarithms and using \(\log j\le j\).
Consequently the following are eventually exact:
\[
 \begin{array}{ll}
 \Psi_6(12\xi_j)=P_3j^{-12},&
 \Psi_6(16\xi_j)=P_4j^{-12},\\
 \Theta_1(8\xi_j)=T_{1,2}j^{-4},&
 \Theta_1(12\xi_j)=T_{1,3}j^{-4},\\
 \Theta_6^{\rm vec}(12\xi_j)=T_{6,3}j^{-12},&
 \Theta_6^{\rm vec}(16\xi_j)=T_{6,4}j^{-12}.
 \end{array}\tag{PG65}
\]
The factor two in the exponential branch of \(\Psi_6\) changes
none of these eventual equalities.

To retain all summands, write \(B_6=B_{6,\partial}+B_{6,{\rm int}}\)
and \(A_D=A_{D,\partial}+A_{D,{\rm int}}\) according to their
two displayed brackets. Write the odd bound as
\(A_F=A_{F,\partial,0}+A_{F,\partial,1}
+A_{F,{\rm int},0}+A_{F,{\rm int},1}\), where index zero
selects the factor one and index one selects the displayed
\(\Theta_1/2\). Substitution of (PG65), \(n_j/j^4\to2\),
\(J_{n_j}/j^4\to1\), and both cosine limits gives the complete
table of nonzero leading coefficients:
\[
 \begin{array}{c|c|c}
 \text{exact positive summand}&\text{power }p&
                 \lim_{j\to\infty}j^p(\text{summand})\\ \hline
 B_{6,\partial}&32&(3/16)T_{6,3}\\
 B_{6,{\rm int}}&28&(3/32)T_{6,4}\\
 A_{D,\partial}&32&(3/16)P_3T_{1,2}\\
 A_{D,{\rm int}}&28&(3/32)P_4T_{1,3}\\
 A_{F,\partial,0}&28&P_3/8\\
 A_{F,\partial,1}&32&P_3T_{1,2}/16\\
 A_{F,{\rm int},0}&24&P_4/16\\
 A_{F,{\rm int},1}&28&P_4T_{1,3}/32
 \end{array}\tag{PG66}
\]
Each row is an asymptotic statement about its displayed exact
summand. The table does not discard finite-size corrections of
other summands or assert a multi-term expansion of the total.
For example \(3J_n^2/n^5\) times \(j^{12}\) tends to \(3/32\),
and \(2J_n^2/n^5\) times \(j^{12}\) tends to \(1/16\);
these prove the interior \(A_D\) and \(A_F\) entries. The other
entries follow from the same products with one less \(J_n\).

It follows that the final lower-bound functions satisfy
\[
 \begin{aligned}
 j^{28}(B_{6,j}+A_{D,j})
       &\longrightarrow\frac3{32}(T_{6,4}+P_4T_{1,3}),\\
 j^{24}(B_{6,j}+A_{F,j})
       &\longrightarrow P_4/16,\\
 j^{30}\kappa_*\beta_{0,j}^2(B_{6,j}+A_{F,j})
       &\longrightarrow\frac{625\pi^2}{2\kappa_*}P_4,\\
 j^{34}\kappa_*\beta_{0,j}^2(B_{6,j}+A_{D,j})
       &\longrightarrow\frac{1875\pi^2}{4\kappa_*}
                                      (T_{6,4}+P_4T_{1,3}) .
 \end{aligned}\tag{PG67}
\]
Here \(j^6\beta_{0,j}^2\to5000\pi^2/\kappa_*^2\).
In the last line the quantity bounded below is the energy of
the unchanged even raw state \(\beta_0D\psi\); no division by
its mass is made.

The complete endpoint also has the exact even identity
\[
 {\cal E}(s)=
 4\beta_0^2[Q_4-(\kappa/s)K_1]
          -\beta_0^2\mu(D^2)
          +(\kappa\beta_0^2/s)\mu(|\nabla D|^2).
 \tag{PG68}
\]
Indeed, expanding the full squares gives
\(\mu(D^2)=2(Q_4+Q_4^{\mathcal R})\) and
\(\mu(|\nabla D|^2)=2(K_1+K_{\mathcal R})\);
inserting them gives exactly (PG45). Thus the strengthened
original-path receiving statements, with their signs intact, are
\[
 \boxed{\begin{aligned}
 {\cal E}_j(s_j)&\le
      G_j-j\beta_{0,j}^2(B_{6,j}+A_{F,j}),\\
 {\cal E}_j(s_j)&\ge
      4\beta_{0,j}^2[Q_{4,j}-jK_{1,j}]
       -\beta_{0,j}^2\mu_j(D_j^2)
       +j\beta_{0,j}^2(B_{6,j}+A_{D,j}).
 \end{aligned}}\tag{PG69}
\]
Their explicit negative and positive contributions have the limits
\[
 \begin{aligned}
 \lim_{j\to\infty}j^{29}
       j\beta_{0,j}^2(B_{6,j}+A_{F,j})
           &=\frac{625\pi^2}{2\kappa_*^2}P_4,\\
 \lim_{j\to\infty}j^{33}
       j\beta_{0,j}^2(B_{6,j}+A_{D,j})
           &=\frac{1875\pi^2}{4\kappa_*^2}
                                      (T_{6,4}+P_4T_{1,3}) .
 \end{aligned}\tag{PG70}
\]
The positive correction has the additional strictly positive
coefficient \(P_4T_{1,3}\) beyond the boundary-only leading
coefficient. The raw odd energy has the bulk leading power
\(j^{-30}\), with every boundary contribution still retained.
The remaining \(G_j\), \(Q_{4,j}-jK_{1,j}\), and \(\mu_j(D_j^2)\)
are the original expectations, not placeholders replaced by
assumptions. Their signs in (PG69) forbid substituting lower mass
bounds into negative mass terms to infer a lower endpoint bound.
No energy-to-mass quotient or positive limiting spectral-window
mass is concluded.
